# nl-ns namespace-boundary baseline (tools/nl-ns-inventory.el).
# One "KIND COUNT" line per tracked finding kind.  Raise a number only
# in the commit that introduces the finding; lower it whenever the
# ratchet notice says you can.
#
# ns-collision-divergent is the actionable half: the definitions differ,
# so which behaviour you get depends on load order.  ns-collision is the
# benign half: same definition in two places.
#
# 2026-08-17 catch-up.  This gate was never wired into CI, so nobody ran
# it between 2026-08-15 and today and every number below had drifted:
#
#   ns-collision            275 -> 148   (-127, captured)
#   ns-collision-divergent  114 ->  97   ( -17, captured)
#   ns-prefix-violation     853 -> 848   (  -5, captured)
#   ns-private-escape       526 -> 611   ( +85, conceded)
#
# Four of the five move in the tightening direction and are locked in
# here, so they cannot grow back.  The fifth is conceded rather than
# fixed: the escapes are concentrated -- 352 of the 611 are in
# scripts/compile-elisp-objects.el (242) and
# scripts/nelisp-standalone-build.el (110), with
# lisp/nelisp-aot-compiler.el next at 40 -- and unpicking a private-name
# boundary inside the build path is a change that wants its own commit
# and its own verification, not a drive-by.
#
# The step that keeps this honest went in with the same commit:
# ns-inventory now runs in CI beside unsafe-inventory.  A drift of 85
# findings should not have been able to accumulate unnoticed, and from
# here it cannot.
#
# 848 -> 850 on 2026-08-19.  `nl-ns-accepted-note' and
# `nl-ns-unnoted-accepted' are two new public entry points in
# packages/nl-ns/src/nl-ns.el, added so the accepted-divergence file can
# carry a per-entry reason.  All 25 of that file's public functions are
# counted here, the 23 that predate today included: the check wants
# `nl-ns--' for every name that is not the feature name itself, so what
# it measures in practice is public API surface, which is most of what
# the 848 is.
#
# Renaming them was measured rather than assumed, and does not remove the
# finding -- it moves it:
#
#   nl-ns-  (as committed)   prefix-violation 25   private-escape 0
#   nl-ns--                  prefix-violation 23   private-escape 1
#
# because scripts/nl-ns-gate.el calls one of them across the boundary.
# That trades two findings in the kind this file is tightening (853 ->
# 848) for one in the kind it concedes (611, growing).  The two names
# also belong to a family -- load-accepted, write-accepted,
# stale-accepted, unaccepted -- that is public single-dash throughout,
# and splitting the family's naming to move a number is the wrong way
# round.
# 148 -> 149 on 2026-08-19: `nelisp--path-split', the component splitter
# `expand-file-name' needs.  It is duplicated into lisp/nelisp-stdlib-misc.el
# beside the `expand-file-name' that calls it, because leaving it only in the
# prelude made that file reference a private name it does not define -- an
# ns-private-escape, which is the finding kind this tree is conceding rather
# than tightening.  A benign same-definition collision in the kind that is
# tightening is the better of the two, and it matches what the file already
# does: `expand-file-name' itself is duplicated there on purpose, and both
# copies now carry a note saying `make ns-gate' polices the drift.
# 149 -> 150 on 2026-08-19: `compare-strings'.  It existed only in
# lisp/nelisp-stdlib-plist-str.el, which the standalone does not load, so it
# was `void-function' at runtime -- the body was fine and simply in the
# wrong place.  Moving a copy to the prelude makes it exist where it runs;
# the two are byte-identical, so this is the benign same-definition kind and
# ns-collision-divergent stays at 97.
# 150 -> 152 on 2026-08-19: `lsh' and `copy-sequence'.  `lsh' did not exist
# in the standalone at all (void-function) and lisp/nelisp-stdlib-misc.el
# aliased it to `ash', which is a different operation -- a right shift of a
# negative value fills with the sign bit there and with zeros here.  Both
# files now carry the real definition, byte-identical, so this is the benign
# same-definition kind.
# 152 -> 156 on 2026-08-19: `print-length', `print-level',
# `nelisp--prn-depth' and `nelisp--prn-symbol-needs-leading-escape-p'.  The
# printer gained `print-length'/`print-level' support and a symbol escaper
# that looks at the whole name, and lisp/nelisp-stdlib-prn.el carries the
# same bodies so the two do not drift.  Same-definition, so benign.
# 156 -> 157 on 2026-08-19: `round' and `intern-soft', which moved OUT of
# ns-collision-divergent (98 -> 96) rather than being new duplicates.
# `round' did not exist in the standalone at all, and the lisp/ copy routed
# it through libm round(), which rounds a half AWAY FROM ZERO -- so it
# answered 1 for (round 0.5) where Emacs answers 0.  `intern-soft' existed
# only in lisp/nelisp-stdlib-misc.el, which the standalone does not load, so
# it was void-function there.  Both files now carry one Emacs-correct text,
# which is the trade this gate asks for: a benign same-definition collision
# in place of a divergence whose behaviour depended on load order.
# 157 -> 161 on 2026-08-19: `string-to-number' and the two helpers it needs
# (`nelisp-stdlib--whitespace-p', `nelisp-stdlib--digit-value'), plus
# `nelisp-pcase--distribute-or'.  The parser lived only in
# lisp/nelisp-stdlib-plist-str.el, which the standalone never loads, so the
# native integer-only path answered instead: "1.5" came back 1 and
# (string-to-number "ff" 16) came back 0, because RADIX had nowhere to go.
# Same shape as `compare-strings' and `lsh' before it -- the body was right
# and simply in the wrong place.  All four are byte-identical across the two
# files, so this is the benign same-definition kind and `make ns-gate' is
# what keeps them that way.
# 161 -> 162 on 2026-08-20: `sequencep'.  It was void in the standalone --
# (sequencep 0) answered `void-function' where Emacs answers nil -- and the
# body already existed in lisp/nelisp-stdlib.el, which the standalone does
# not load.  Same shape as `compare-strings', `lsh', `string-to-number'
# before it: the code was right and in the wrong place.  Byte-identical in
# both files, so this is the benign same-definition kind.
# 162 -> 163 on 2026-08-20: `nelisp--check-string', the one place that
# decides what "this argument must be a string" means.  Four string stubs and
# five string functions were signalling `sequencep' -- what `length' checks --
# where Emacs signals `stringp', so a handler written for the documented
# condition never fired.  It lives in both files because the prelude and
# lisp/nelisp-stdlib-misc.el each carry copies of the functions that use it;
# byte-identical, so this is the benign same-definition kind and `make
# ns-gate' is what keeps it that way.
# 163 -> 164 on 2026-08-20: `nelisp--check-symbol', the symbol counterpart of
# `nelisp--check-string'.  `get' and `boundp' answered nil for a non-symbol,
# which is also the answer for a symbol that has no such property or binding,
# and `fmakunbound'/`defalias'/`fset' answered their argument.  Byte-identical
# where it is duplicated, so this is the benign same-definition kind.
# 164 -> 165 on 2026-08-20: `nelisp--check-plist' and the helpers that
# followed the coding-system stubs into lisp/nelisp-stdlib-misc.el.  Each is
# byte-identical to its prelude copy -- `make ns-gate' named all three the
# moment they diverged, which is the whole point of duplicating the text
# rather than the intent.
# 165 -> 167 on 2026-08-20: `nelisp--check-list' and `nelisp--check-number',
# following `assoc'/`member' and the numeric wrappers into the lisp/ mirrors.
# One helper per PREDICATE is the point: Emacs names a specific predicate for
# each requirement and a handler matches on that name, so a single generic
# "bad type" check would be no better than none.  Byte-identical copies, so
# this is the benign same-definition kind.
# 167 -> 169 on 2026-08-20: `file-truename', `buffer-file-name',
# `make-bool-vector' and `log' were absent from the standalone and are now
# in both the prelude and the lisp/ mirror that already named them, plus the
# `nelisp--check-string' helper following `file-truename' across.  All
# byte-identical, so this is the benign same-definition kind.
# 169 -> 170 on 2026-08-20: `path-separator', which
# packages/nelisp-process/src/nelisp-process.el already declared -- but the
# standalone does not load that package, so the variable was void there.
# Byte-identical, benign same-definition.  171 also covers `bool-vector',
# which the standalone did not have.
# 171 -> 175 on 2026-08-20: `bool-vector-p', `point-min', `point-max' and
# `locate-library', absent from the standalone and now in both the prelude
# and the lisp/ mirror that already named them.  Byte-identical copies.
# 176 -> 178 on 2026-08-20: `file-truename' and `mod', each brought into
# line with its lisp/ mirror rather than left to differ.  Byte-identical, so
# this is the benign same-definition kind.
# 178 -> 176 on 2026-08-20, ratcheted DOWN: `byte-compile-file' lost its
# prelude stub (src/nelisp-bytecode.el has the real one and the standalone
# does not load it, so a stub would shadow it on the host), and the count
# followed.  Lowering it in the commit that shrank the surface is what the
# ratchet notice asks for.
# 177 -> 178 on 2026-08-20: `equal-including-properties', absent from the
# standalone and aliased to `equal' in both files -- there are no text
# properties here, so the two are the same function.
#
# 178 -> 157 on 2026-08-21: lowered to the measurement, same day the
# `pinned-collision' identities below were added -- a ceiling 21 above the
# count it is meant to bound is not a ceiling, the same finding that moved
# `tools/parity-coverage-baseline.txt' from 259 to its measured floor.
# 180 -> 225 on 2026-08-23 (Doc 188 P1, buffer unification): 49 new
# collisions from porting src/nelisp-buffer.el's object model verbatim
# into scripts/nelisp-stdlib-prelude.el, offset by 4 collisions that
# disappeared (the misc.el copies of `bufferp'/`goto-char'/`insert'/
# `insert-file-contents' were deleted, not synced, so those pairs no
# longer collide at all).  Each of the 49 has its own `pinned-collision'

# 990 -> 994 on 2026-09-12: `nelisp-native-unit-development-timeout' and

# also tips this file's majority prefix from `nelisp-native-unit-' to
# `nelisp-native-unit-development-', which newly catches the pre-existing
# `nelisp-native-unit-rebuild-and-reload' as a fourth finding -- same
# symbol, same public/private status as before this commit, no behaviour
# change; only the majority-prefix inference around it shifted. No
# existing symbol is redefined.

# 999 -> 1006 on 2026-09-12: Doc 202's lisp/nelisp-native-callsite.el public

# `-reachability' and its declared constants.  Counted the same way every
# other public entry in a `--'-namespaced module is; `-reachability' in
# particular is the API that stops this branch over-claiming which callers a
# replacement reaches, so hiding it behind the private prefix is the wrong
# trade.
# Private names stop namespace inference at the first `--'. This removes
# false public-API violations in helper-heavy files; foreign prefixes and
# cross-file private references remain checked. Measured on 404 files.

# `pinned-collision KEY' pins every ns-collision and ns-collision-divergent
# finding by identity, KEY in `nl-ns-finding-key' shape (kind + subject +
# sorted files, plus a 12-hex digest of the definitions when divergent --
# the same `:shape' that fixed ns-gate on 2026-08-21).  Added the same day,
# same reason: `ns-collision' above just dropped from 178 to 157 measured,
# and a per-kind count cannot see one collision resolve while a different
# one appears, net unchanged -- `gate-mutation' demonstrated exactly that
# shape of blind spot against `ns-gate' and `parity-coverage' this session.
# A pinned key disappearing is still green; a collision with no pin at all
# fails and names it.  `ns-prefix-violation' and `ns-private-escape' stay
# count-only -- see the header comment in tools/nl-ns-inventory.el for why.
# Generated from a clean tree; do not hand-edit into drift.
#
# Doc segI (vendor-emacs-lisp) gate-widening fix (2026-09-21, same day):
# deleting the WAVE-2-era guarded `cl-remove-if-not' in scripts/nelisp-
# stdlib-prelude.el (a hand-written, keyword-ignoring subset of
# vendor/emacs-lisp/emacs-lisp/cl-seq.el's real `cl-remove-if-not', the
# same defect class as the `cl-count'/`cl-assoc' pair this fix also
# removes) leaves only that file's UNCONDITIONAL `cl-remove-if-not' (a
# separate, already-`vendor-shadow-accepted.txt'-accepted 2-arg helper
# used by nelisp-aot-compiler.el), which no longer differs in body shape
# from lisp/nelisp-cl-macros.el's own copy -- so `ns-collision-divergent'
# downgrades to a plain `ns-collision', needing its own pin here instead.
#
# 2026-11-07 catch-up after the 3a2862816 merge (feat/emacs-lib-import:
# the Doc 211 emacs-lib import plus the core lanes).  Neither side re-ran
# this gate; the core-lane tip 0a618c89c alone already failed it with 1293
# unpinned collisions, so the growth is lane bookkeeping, not merge damage:
#
#   ns-collision            265 ->  433   (+168, conceded)
#   ns-collision-divergent  128 ->  897   (+769, conceded)
#   ns-prefix-violation     329 -> 2420   (+2091, conceded)
#   ns-private-escape       852 -> 2389   (+1537, conceded)
#
# The 1170 new collisions are pinned below by identity.  Most pair
# packages/emacs-lib-core/src with scripts/nelisp-stdlib-prelude.el or
# lisp/: both define the same standard name, and for the divergent half
# which body wins depends on load order (the dual buffer family fixed in
# ca0a70fd4 was one of them).  Pinning stops new ones; removing the
# duplicates is tracked as its own objective, not done here.
